# 20 — Orchestration: the board

**Current state only.** History is `docs/17-DECISION-LEDGER.md` (what was
decided, and why); seams are `docs/18-ARCHITECTURE.md` (how the code works).
This file is the chief of staff's memory that outlives its own session
(`AGENTS.md` §Chief of staff): in-flight writers, unlanded branches, and the
owner's decision queue. It is **overwritten in place, never appended**, and it
must stay ONE SCREEN — a record that no longer describes the present belongs in
`docs/17` (if it decided something) or nowhere.

The PROCESS this board serves — roles, record vocabulary, the gate, the
verification doctrine, the brief template and the porting checklist — is
described self-containedly in `docs/22-DEVELOPMENT-PROCESS.md`.

## The contract

1. **Record grammar: one line per fact, stable prefix, `field=value` pairs.**
   `grep '^IN-FLIGHT' docs/20-ORCHESTRATION.md` is the query, and the answer is
   one line — not a prose block to ingest.
2. **Updated in the same commit as the landing it records** (the `docs/18` §0
   rule), and **true before the CoS reports a landing to the owner**: if the
   session dies the second after that report, a successor must be able to act
   from nothing but this file, `docs/17`, and git.
3. **Every record names something checkable** — branch, worktree, session id,
   SHA, path. Prose about state rots; a claim that can be tested does not.
4. **The reconciliation is the enforcement.** No test can see branches or
   sessions; `bash scripts/board.sh` checks every claim below against git, the
   session directories and the host, and finishes with `BOARD RECONCILED` or
   `BOARD STALE`. Session start runs it before anything is dispatched.
5. **A SESSION and an IN-FLIGHT record name the MODEL** (`model=<provider>/<model>`).
   A plain `subagent`/`subagent_fork` inherits the session's route, so that is
   what it says; a worker dispatched through a `workflow` can carry a different
   one, and the record says which, because a landing's quality is only comparable
   across experiments if the model that produced it is on the record.

(a) **THE COPY-ONLY KEY'S SCHEMA CARRIED A TRANSFORM, SO THE CONVERSION ITSELF THREW**: `copiedMobSpellAssignmentSchema` used the transforming `spellDataSchema` (which stamps `filterAxis`), and `z.toJSONSchema` cannot represent a transform AT ALL — `StrictSchemaError: schema "statblock" cannot be converted … Transforms cannot be represented in JSON Schema`, which took the encounter-brief contract and the stat-block caster-field pin down with it. My first attempt dropped the key from the EMITTED schema (the `extras` precedent) and re-ran: the SAME six failed, because the throw happens before any dropping. The real fix is at the SCHEMA — the copy key now uses a TRANSFORM-FREE object schema (`storedSpellDataSchema`), the shape a copy is actually stored in, with `filterAxis` already stamped by the library parse that produced it; the emit-side drop STAYS, because a prompt must never be asked to author a library payload. (b) **THE READY-BOOK RULE WAS RE-SPELLED, AND MY FIRST FIX WAS WRONG TOO**: the new tx-callable spell lookup inside `db/mobCopyRepair` spelled the filter again — and the two spellings already DISAGREED (the rule's owner says `(system === undefined || book.system === system)`, the copy said hard equality), which is exactly how a second copy drifts. I first extracted a named boolean predicate, and the pin red with `expected [] to deeply equal ['src/db/rulebookRepo.ts']` because its needle IS the literal arrow `(book) => book.status === 'ready'` — the spelling is what the pin names. The rule is now a shared FILTER, `readyBooksOf(books, system)`, keeping that arrow in its one home while both callers go through it. **FIFTH time this session a focused set was exactly the set that could not see its own break** (the landing adjusted ONE compatibility pin by name and never ran the strict-schema family) — and the two failed attempts are the same lesson applied to the dispatcher's own work: a fix that is "obviously" the right shape still has to be run against the pin that owns the behaviour. THE FIXED TREE IS GREEN: the dispatcher's suite on the tree carrying `0439d9b` came back **356 files / 4569 tests, 7/7 chunks, 0 voided, lint 0 errors, typecheck clean, combined peak 2596 MB of the 3000 MB cap, wall 572s** (raw log `.gate-logs/gate-983779-20260919T214147-26435`), which is the evidence that all SIX failures are closed rather than merely absent from a focused run.

DECIDED-OWNER | id=no-regex-on-free-text | decision=**FREE TEXT IS READ BY THE MODEL, NEVER BY A PATTERN — AND IF A VALUE MUST BE EXACT, THE FORM BECOMES STRUCTURED** (owner, 2026-09-20, verbatim: *"Just as a GENERAL rule: NEVER expect a free text to confirm to any regexable pattern. Thats a design disaster. If structured input is needed, the form can never be free and needs to be structured as well. Unstructured text needs an LLM. Period. If there are more such occurrance, this needs to be fixed as well. Scan for regexes in this region."*). THE MEASURED CASE: the owner typed *'…images are preserved, but level needs to be bumped to 3.'* into the instruction box; the level reader is a regex requiring the number ADJACENT to a level word (`level 3`), his sentence read as NO level, the chain fell to the minted block's level 1, and the stat block was bound there while the PROSE model — which reads the sentence itself — wrote the level he asked for (partial success, docs/17 row 289). THE DISTINCTION THAT DECIDES A VIOLATION: a pattern over a STRUCTURED FIELD or a format the app owns (a `levelBand`, a `[[Name|alias]]` link, a JSON pack payload, an ISO date) is FINE; a pattern over the app's OWN machine-generated line is FINE but must stay paired with the formatter that writes it; a pattern over HUMAN OR MODEL FREE TEXT (an instruction, a brief, module prose, a stat block narrative, a typed hint) is a DEFECT. THE OBLIGATION: read it with a structured zod-validated MODEL call bounded to the domain and answering `null` honestly when the text asks for nothing, and NAME what was read on the user-visible surface so a wrong read is correctable in one step; if the value must be exact, the FORM becomes structured (a number field / select) and the free text stops being the input. WIDENING THE PATTERN IS NOT A FIX — human phrasing is unbounded and multi-language. A regex may remain as a FAST PRE-READ for a machine-clean form (`level 5`) only where the model read is the authority. THE RULE IS BINDING IN `AGENTS.md` (engineering rule 5) and the whole tree is in scope: a read-only SCAN is dispatched to enumerate and price every pattern that reads free text, and its findings become queued rows rather than silent debt. | date=2026-09-20
DECIDED-OWNER | id=clean-cut-no-migrations | decision=**ABOLISH THE MIGRATION LAYER — A CLEAN CUT THAT REFUSES OLD DATA INSTEAD OF MIGRATING IT** (owner, 2026-09-20, verbatim: *"since this app is still in test phase, losing data FOR NOW is no big deal, modules can be recreated... So, no need to go big into migration. We could even make a clean cut here and refuse to load older versions (make a version bump) if that is much cleaner. Right now i would rather like to abolish any migration code to get to a clean base."*). **WHAT IT COLLAPSES:** the `unconvertible-row-isolation-fork` AWAITING-OWNER (no migration means no unconvertible rows, so all three of its arms die), the row-248 TYPE fork AND its module-arm fork (delete the legacy schema arms and ONE live type remains; there is nothing left to represent), the v24 mob-copy migration, the legacy READ seam, the start-up retry, the import manifest legacy blocking arms, and the migration-notice machinery. **WHAT IT DOES NOT TOUCH, because it is NOT migration:** crash recovery (PDF / pack / run / moduleGen reconcile, rows 266 and 277), the copy-on-write adoption model (rows 255-271), the backup/restore MECHANISM (row 265), rows 276/277 in flight, and row 234 — a literal reading of "abolish any migration code" that deletes those would be a DEFECT, not compliance. **THE MECHANISM, CORRECTED BEFORE IT WAS BRIEFED:** a version bump ALONE does not refuse anything — Dexie applies the new schema to an older database and carries the surviving rows forward, which IS the fragmentation he wants gone; the refusal has to be EXPLICIT (probe `211356c8` verifies this against the installed Dexie source and prices two gates: a NEW DATABASE NAME plus `deleteDatabase`, versus ONE declared version plus a versionless-open start-up gate). **THE PRICE, NAMED:** the database in his browser today, and every backup or export taken before the cut, become unloadable — accepted by the owner for the test phase. | date=2026-09-20 **THE TWO SUB-CHOICES, ANSWERED 2026-09-20** (owner, verbatim: *"Ok, lets do that then. Code stability right now trumps compatibility. That changes back once these changes are in place."*): (1) the old browser database is DELETED when the cut lands, not merely refused, behind a LOUD named screen that says what was removed; (2) pre-cut backups and export files are REFUSED loudly, because a restore is a migration in disguise; (3) **THE AMNESTY IS ONE-TIME AND BOUNDED** — compatibility discipline RESUMES the moment this landing is in place: after the clean base a schema change owes a migration again, and silent data loss stays forbidden (AGENTS rule 1). The DELETED-because-blocked case (`indexedDB.deleteDatabase` refusing while another tab holds the database open) is named as its own behaviour and pinned by the probe. **REFINED BY THE OWNER 2026-09-20** (verbatim: *"Make sure that after the changes, old campaigns get cleanly deleted. I think the other settings can survive, right? I just don't want to get stuck in an error state."*): the cut is a SELECTIVE PURGE, NOT a whole-database delete — every CAMPAIGN-scoped store is cleared cleanly, while the LIBRARY (rulebooks / chunks / embeddings, which cost a re-fetch to rebuild) and the app SETTINGS SURVIVE. This SUPERSEDES the delete-the-whole-database arm recorded above, and with it the `deleteDatabase`/`onblocked` hazard: NO database delete happens on the main path at all. The purge runs BEFORE any campaign row can be parsed, so no old shape ever reaches a reader and the app cannot land in an error state; it ends in ONE loud non-blocking notice naming what was removed (with a count) and what was kept — never a blocking gate the owner can be stuck behind. SETTINGS survive except that rows which carried the migration report lose only those fields, because the machinery that wrote them is deleted with the rest. The ONE residual hazard is a stored database NEWER than the code declares (Dexie `VersionError`), which keeps its own priced fallback. Pre-cut backups stay REFUSED loudly. Probe `211356c8` has been STEERED with this (store-by-store clear/keep classification, the purge-before-read rule, the no-error-state criterion, and the pins). **OWNER CLARIFICATION 2026-09-20, on how the cut is COMPLETED — COMPAT TESTS DIE, THEY ARE NOT REWRITTEN** (verbatim: *"If a test is just there for compatibility behaviour, that can die and rest in peace, since we retire that."*). This overrides the dispatcher first phrasing ("a surviving rule gets rewritten, never deleted"), which was too conservative and would have kept compat-only pins alive as rewritten tests: the triage is by **SUBJECT, NOT COLOUR**. A test whose SUBJECT is a RETIRED compatibility behaviour (a `rulebook` chunk resolving to a page label, the `creatureRef` borrowed-stats path, migration/repair semantics, the legacy source arms) is DELETED outright — no rewrite, no replacement, no tombstone — and a whole file whose subject is retired compatibility is deleted with it. A test whose SUBJECT is a LIVE rule but whose FIXTURE was legacy (identity keys, portrait lanes, seed freezing, adopted-copy stamps, the re-homed `npc-ref` resolution, settings/library survival) keeps its assertion with the fixture converted. And a red test whose subject is LIVE but whose assertion no longer holds is a REAL DEFECT, fixed in code and reported, never deleted. Nothing dies merely for being red; nothing compat-only is resurrected.
DECIDED-OWNER | id=one-level-source-of-truth | decision=**A MOB HAS ONE LEVEL, AND IT IS THE ONE ITS STATS SAY** (owner, 2026-09-20, verbatim: *"Whatever the problem, even if its a misread, thats even worse. Because we have 2 places that disagree on level. The NPC smith correctly minted level 1 mobs. There should be only 1 source of truth about a mob level."*). **WHAT IT RULES OUT:** a module entity `levelHint` PRESENTED AS A FACT about the character; two numbers answering one question; a chip that can disagree with the character it names. **WHAT IT IMPLIES:** the chip reads the MINTED stat block; the hint is a GENERATION INPUT (kept — a level stated only in the prose is otherwise lost at the entity boundary, docs/17 row 247) and is never displayed as the mob level; a stored hint that disagrees with an EXISTING block is reconciled or reported LOUDLY (AGENTS rule 1), never two silent numbers; and a FABRICATED hint still matters even once the display is fixed, because it steers FUTURE generations. **EVIDENCE:** a levels-1-2 pf2e module whose EVERY npc chip read `level 7` while the Smith minted level 1 — the chip is stamped from the premise via `firstLevelInText` + `withCombatEntityLevelHints`. Slice reserved **282**. | date=2026-09-20 **SHARPENED BY THE OWNER 2026-09-20** (verbatim: *"Oh yea, in the premise i mention a level 7 gnome (that is not supposed to be an opponent so early). Its very sloppy to infer all mobs levels from a CAMPAIGN premise, thats not even module instructive. Its a premise for the whole CAMPAIGN. And this was about 1 NPC."*): **THE PREMISE IS NOT A LEVEL SOURCE AT ALL.** A narrative premise is a story introduction, not a level instruction, and the sentence that produced the 7 was about a SINGLE npc. So the fix is NOT "restrict the premise read to ranges" but "REMOVE IT": a MODULE-WIDE level may come only from STRUCTURED data (an EXACT band); per-entity levels come from the part’s exact level BY NAME and the model’s own structured per-entity `levelHint` — which is NAME-SCOPED, and is where his gnome 7 belongs; `instructionLevel` (the owner speaking explicitly about one entity) is UNAFFECTED and keeps the text reader. **THIS DELIBERATELY REVERSES PART OF docs/17 row 247**, whose level-5-smith case is now served by those channels instead of a prose scan; the reversal and its reason are recorded in row 282, and the honest TRADE is reported rather than glossed (an entity whose level lives ONLY in prose and which the model did not hint falls to the module band).

Significance is a narrative judgement, and a table would be a SECOND source of truth about exactly the thing this arc removes. The band governs the BALANCE of ENCOUNTER PARTICIPANTS only. "Realistic" is the model judgement (standing, role, age), calibrated by the owner negative example: a fourteen-year-old is never level 10. **CONSEQUENCE FOR (4): the out-of-band ADVISORY applies ONLY to a figure that TAKES PART IN AN ENCOUNTER** (where being out of band is a balance problem); for a non-combat figure an out-of-band level is EXPECTED and must raise NO advisory. Read-only: no tests, no gate, no lock, tree left clean. **RESULT — FOUR LANDMINES AND ONE CONFLICT.** (1) THE OWNER MECHANISM IS A NO-OP, proven in installed Dexie 4.4.5: fewer declared versions opens the STORED version instead (`dexie.js:4599-4604` catches `VersionError` and retries versionless, `:4008` overwrites `db.verno`, `:4013-4018` checks only for MISSING stores), so a version bump silently carries every old row forward — this is the measured confirmation of the correction recorded above. Kept: the drift policy (docs/17 row 261) is current cross-machine behaviour, NOT file-version compat. Loud unknowns in the report §e, the load-bearing one being that no typecheck/lint/suite was run, so the fixture ripple is unenumerated. **STEER-2 OUTCOME — THE MECHANISM IS DECIDED: C.** Keep the name `campaigner` and declare ONE `version(31)` (> the stored 30) whose `.upgrade()` body transactionally purges the CAMPAIGN-scoped stores, verified in installed Dexie 4.4.5: `:4512 verno*10` = 310, `:4526` opens at 310, `:4546 oldVer` 300, `:3815 versToRun = [v31]`, `:3844-3845` runs and AWAITS the body — and `:3816-3818` is the trap, because with only `version(1)` declared `versToRun` is EMPTY and NOTHING runs (the new #1 most-likely miss; the old #1, the database NAME, no longer applies to C). A FAILS the owner criteria: the LIBRARY and `settings` both live in `campaigner`, `openRouterApiKey` included, so he re-enters the key and re-fetches and re-embeds every pack. A-prime passes them only by re-importing old-shape chunks through a NEW cross-database copy plus a resumability marker plus a blocked-delete arm, at an estimated ~4.1 MB per bestiary book (492 chunks x ~2 KB text + 492 x ~6.1 KB embeddings; basis docs/12:758-760 and a 1536-dim model — an ESTIMATE, his database is unreadable from here); C copies 0 bytes. **STORE TABLE (report §f.2, all 17 stores):** CLEAR = campaigns, modules, battles, runs, moduleVersions, creatureImages; SELECTIVE = artifacts and images on `campaignId !== null` (`null` rows ARE the published library and the `mobPortraits` blobs) and revisions with a non-surviving artifactId; KEEP = rulebooks, chunks, embeddings, pdfFiles, personas, ideaBoards, mobPortraits, settings. WARNING recorded: a `campaignId: null` row is INVISIBLE to its own index (IndexedDB keys cannot be null), so it must be read `toArray()` and filtered, never `where(campaignId).equals(null)`. **SETTINGS STRICTNESS IS NOT A HAZARD** (no `.strict()` anywhere in `settings.ts`/`settingsRepo.ts`, zod strips the six deleted report fields, and the row is merged OVER the defaults) — pinned anyway, because it is one `.strict()` away from the forbidden error state. **A FAILED PURGE CANNOT WEDGE BY DATA:** Dexie aborts the whole versionchange transaction atomically (`dexie.js:3745`, `:3756`, `:3764`), so the version stays 30 with every row intact and a reload is a clean retry; a `try`/`catch` in the body is FORBIDDEN (it would swallow a real error AND commit a partial purge). The loud surface to ADD is a named recovery card ("nothing was removed", the error text, Reload) plus a NON-BLOCKING line for a blocked upgrade from another tab. 12 pins in §f.7. **THE REMAINING FORK (§f.9), decided on the probe recommendation:** if the surviving library still holds an unconvertible `rulebook`/`creatureRef` spelling, NORMALISE it inside the same purge transaction (~15 lines, counted in the notice) instead of keeping the arms inert and deferring the deletion — the purge count `libraryLegacyCitationsDropped` is the instrument that reveals it on the first launch. Pre-cut files stay REFUSED (free under C: the browser library already survives, so an old backup adds nothing). `plural.ts` is KEPT (the new `formatCleanCut` needs it).

Trace, with file:line: the chip (`features/modules/entity-panel.tsx:1386`, `entityLevelHintFor`) back to its two writers (the spine planner answer, and `domain/module.withCombatEntityLevelHints` fed by `roomBudget.moduleStatedLevel` — at spine time the PREMISE only), the premise reader (`firstLevelInText` + `language.levelWordsPattern`, an ELEVEN-language word union with `\s*(\d{1,2})`) and its false-positive space, whether the stamped hint reaches the generators at all (the precedence chain: user instruction > record `levelHint` > `moduleStatedLevel` > brief), whether the clean cut or rows 247/253 are implicated, every existing pin and which half is UNPINNED, and — STEERED mid-flight with the owner ruling above — where the ONE source of truth must live, what the chip says with no block yet, and what happens when a hint disagrees with a block. **RESULT — THE 7 IS A MODULE-WIDE STAMP FROM A FALSE POSITIVE IN THE PREMISE.** `llm/moduleGen.ts:512` calls `moduleStatedLevel({spine, parts: [], levelMin, levelMax})`; a 1-2 band is a RANGE, so the band yields NOTHING and `firstLevelInText(premise)` (`roomBudget.ts:154`, regex `language.ts:133-136`) is the only source; `domain/module.withCombatEntityLevelHints` (`:832-841`) then stamps that ONE number on EVERY npc that states none, and the chip reads exactly that record — a GENERATION INPUT displayed as a FACT. MEASURED false positives in the regex: a level word used in a RANK/TIER/STEP/FLOOR sense reads its number; `\s*` CROSSES A LINE BREAK (`"auf Stufe\n7"` → 7); `"Stufe7"` with no separator → 7; and ranges misbehave (`"Stufe 1–2"` → 1, `"Stufen 1 bis 2"` → NO MATCH). WHY THE BLOCKS ARE LEVEL 1: the CAST arm (`features/modules/entity-batch.ts:682-695`) writes `statBlock:'none'` and `db/creatureRepo.castCreatureAsNpc` copies the LIBRARY block VERBATIM (`:738`), never reading `levelHint`; a 1-2 window centres on 1.5, so casts land at 1 — while the AUTHORED arm does obey the hint (`runEngine.ts:3860-3873`). **THE CLEAN CUT IS NOT IMPLICATED** (rows 197/247 predate row 278; the cast path never read `levelHint`). UNPINNED: chip-block agreement, and cast-path level vs hint. THE FABRICATED 7 STILL MATTERS once the display is fixed, because it steers FUTURE authored runs. CONFIRMING DATUM, and the owner can supply it in one line: the module stored `spine.premise` — its first level-word+number match is 7.

Enumerate every generation-time level source in EXACT precedence order with file:line; name every specificity INVERSION (entity > part > module); say where the mob own prose lives and whether any reader reads it for a level; settle whether row 282 premise removal is the cause (a REGRESSION) or the ordering defect is older; and enumerate every distinct CLASS of ignored level statement. **RESULT — THE PROSE LOSES BECAUSE THERE IS NO ENTITY-PROSE RUNG, and the last-rung read is SKIPPED WHOLE whenever anything structured resolves.** PRECEDENCE measured at `runEngine.ts:3895-3956`: 1 user instruction (`:3895`) -> 2 minted block (`:3899-3901`) -> 3 stored hint (`:3902`) -> 4 module (`context.moduleGrounding.statedLevel ?? moduleStatedLevel(placementModule, draft.name)`, `:3906-3910`; the part STRUCTURED `levelBand` by name, else the exact band) -> 5 the brief free text (`firstLevelInText(withoutPartyLevelLines(stepBrief))`, `:3954-3955`) — and rung 5 is consulted ONLY when rungs 1-4 resolved nothing (`:3918` + `:3954`). So a mob whose paragraph says level 5 in a part banded 3 ships at 3: INVERSIONS I1 (entity prose vs part band) and I2 (vs the exact module band), both from those two lines. **I3:** with NO minted block, `levelDisagreement` (`:3933-3939`) never fires, so `withoutEntityLevelHintLines` (`:3946-3949`) never strips the "The module fixes this entity's level: N." paragraph while the clause states the instruction number — the prompt then carries TWO levels, the very class row 282 fixed for the block case. **I4:** the fallback read is NOT name-scoped, so the FIRST level anywhere in the brief can win. DELIBERATE, not a defect: block > stored hint (`:3903`). WHERE THE SENTENCE LIVES: the module PART markdown -> `surroundingParagraphs(...)` -> `buildEntityBrief` -> `input.brief`; no reader reads it for a level except that last rung. **ROW 282 DELETED exactly the two premise lines in `moduleStatedLevel` — so IF the sentence is in the PREMISE this IS a regression from the dispatcher own ruling; if it is in the mob paragraph, row 282 changed nothing and the defect is older.** THE ONE SEAM: extend `roomBudget.moduleStatedLevel` (`:253`) with a NAME-SCOPED prose rung via a new pure helper beside `partLevelForMention`, reading the paragraph around the name through the ONE `wikilinks.sentenceAround` + the ONE grammar `firstLevelInText` (no second regex). Honest order: instruction > block > hint > ENTITY-scoped prose (part, then premise, ALWAYS name-scoped) > part band > exact band > bounds. NOT a sixth global source in `runEngine` — that re-opens the module-wide inference the owner banned. CLASSES: A = the cast skip (row 284, in flight); **B/C = the MISSING ENTITY-PROSE RUNG (ONE defect, two winners)**; D = I3 (the prompt carrying two levels under an instruction); E = I4 (the un-name-scoped fallback); F = block > hint (deliberate). I1/I2, I3 and I4 are all UNPINNED.

So please start a discovery in parallel what needs to change for this."*) | row RESERVED **248**, LARGE — **SLICE 1 LANDED** (the v24 migration + stamping; see the LANDED row 248) and the REMAINING SLICE is scoped in docs/18 §5: the MODULE arm (`entityKinds[].bestiary`, needing an in-tx path through the ONE library name-matching seam) plus the pointer-arm deletion for CONVERTED rows | **MOB SIMPLIFICATION: ONE REPRESENTATION — an authored copy of the core stats, pointer and resolution DELETED.** Priced by four read-only probes (data model / consumers / citation machinery / migration), all consumed. **OWNER DECISIONS:** (1) the pointer/citation form is REMOVED, existing refs migrate to copies — one representation, not a deprecation; (2) the SOURCE LINE SURVIVES (a copy still shows "Bestiary p.132"); (3) the NPC **cast-creature** path (`npcDataSchema.creatureRef`) is IN SCOPE, not only roster mobs. **SHAPES TODAY.** Roster mobs carry `monsterEntrySchema.source`, a 4-arm tagged union — `npc-ref{artifactId}` / `inline{statBlock}` / `rulebook{chunkId, contentHash?, creatureName?, bookTitle?}` / `none` — on the encounter artifact's `data.monsters` (artifact.ts:301-335, :351, :500). A SECOND untagged copy of the same pointer sits on NPC rows as `creatureRef` (artifact.ts:231, exclusive with `statBlock` by a refine at :241), and the module side adds a NAME pointer `entityKinds[].bestiary` (module.ts:398-409, :468). Identity is `creatureKey` — `chunk:<id>` for a citation, `content:…` for an authored block (creature.ts:117, :151, :227) — persisted in `mobPortraits`, `creatureImages`, `BattleToken`, `seedFighters`. **THE CRUX, and why this is not a field deletion: THE SOURCE LINE IS NOT STORED.** `creatureOriginLabel` (encounterResolve.ts:187-201) composes it AT READ TIME from a LIVE chunk+rulebook read — `` `${title} p.${chunk.pageStart}` ``, or `` `${title}: ${chunk.headingPath[0]}` `` for a pack-origin book — so `chunkId` is DUAL-purpose (the resolution key AND the only route to the page/heading), and the MIGRATION MUST RESOLVE AND STAMP the composed label (or page+heading) BEFORE dropping the pointer, or "Bestiary p.132" silently degrades. `bookTitle` alone reproduces neither flavour. **DIES (mob-pointer-only):** `resolveCreatureChunk` + BOTH `getChunkByContentHash` injections (encounterResolve.ts:164-172; monsterResolve.ts:21; creatureRepo.ts:83); `contentIdentityFor` (:116) and all four birth sites (runEngine.ts:1521 `rulebookSourceFor`, monster-source.tsx:218, spawn-picker-logic.ts:127, entity-batch.ts:239); `resolveMonsterEntry`'s `rulebook` arm (:539); `creatureRefSchema`/`creatureRefIsEmpty`/`creatureRefForRulebookSource`/`creatureIdentityForCitation`/`libraryCreatureKey` (creature.ts:36, :47, :211, :241, :116); `creatureRepo.resolveCreatureCitation`/`castCreatureAsNpc`; `libraryCitationForEntity`; `creatureCitations.countCreaturesCitedByModule`; `creatureRepair.repairCreatureCitations`; `exportCitationSchema` plus the citation arms of `collectDependencies`/`analyzeDependencies`/`healRulebookSources` and `MissingDependenciesError`'s hash probe. **MUST BE REWRITTEN (where the source line is won or lost):** `rosterReferenceFor` (encounterResolve.ts:404) — its `inline` arm prints NOTHING (:415-419) and a copy will be `inline`, so it must take the STORED attribution; modulePdf.ts:1146-1151, :1223, :1257; pdfExport.ts:233; canvasChat.ts:1455-1473, :1862-1871; monster-source.tsx:64-85, :444-494; BattleSurface.tsx:580-582 (a `chunk:<id>` roster lookup that MISSES for a copy). `statBoxContent`'s optional source parameter already takes a plain string. **SURVIVES:** all chunk identity and everything on it (embeddings, search, spells/items, bestiary/wiki), `rosterStatBlockFor`'s inline arm, `rosterTreasureFor`, `fighterStats.ts`, content-derived identity, and `creatureOriginLabel` demoted to a COPY-TIME formatter. NOTE: `publicationSourceLine` is NOT the mob attribution — it is an ingest-time "Source: Title (LICENSE)" formatter (pf2e-conditions.ts:74, pf2e-rules.ts:128), correcting an earlier guess. **MIGRATION:** a Dexie version bump; the precedent is v20's `repairCreatureCitations` (db.ts:832 → creatureRepair.ts:77), which rewrites and NAMES what it cannot convert rather than migrating silently, surfaced once via settings→AppShell toast. On a resolution failure (pack absent, chunk gone, dangling ref) the honest options are: abort the upgrade loudly, OR convert what resolves and KEEP the failing pointers while naming them — never empty/placeholder stats, never a guessed attribution. NOT reversible: dropping `chunkId` loses which chunk each mob cited. **COSTS AND HAZARDS TO PUT IN EVERY BRIEF:** (a) portrait/image identity breaks — cited mobs key `chunk:<id>`, copies key by content, so existing `creatureImages` rows orphan unless remapped (creature.ts:117/:151/:227; db.ts:928/:930/:1030); (b) "one creature, one look" and cross-encounter sharing END; (c) stat blocks duplicate per instance; (d) docs/12:625 states the OPPOSITE decision ("Nothing is materialized: the chunk stays the single source of truth"), so this arc REVERSES a recorded decision and must amend it; (e) docs/18 §2 rows 108,128,133,134,157,158,160,228 and docs/11 D5/D9 are seam entries to amend in the same landing; (f) pins that go red: `cast-row-borrowed-stats-scan`, `creature-identity-spelling:111`, `encounterReference`, `db/encounterResolve`, `migration.test.ts`, `backup.test.ts`, `creature-identity-one-rule` (`roster-reference-parity` and `pdfExport.test.ts:270` SURVIVE if the formatter stays the composer). **UNMEASURED:** the quantity of pointer-mobs in the owner's live IndexedDB (not readable from here), so the migration's blast radius is unknown until the app counts. **CAST PATH (IN SCOPE) — the measured delta once the NPC pointer is included.** Counts in `src/`: `creatureRef` 60 refs, `castCreatureAsNpc` 13, `isCastCreatureNpc` 14, `npcCreatureRef` 8, `castCreatureWriteRefusal` 7, `BorrowedStatBlock` 5, `derivedStatOrigin` 4. **ADDED TO DIES:** `npcDataSchema.creatureRef` + its mutual-exclusion refine (artifact.ts:231/:241), `castByRunId` (:233), `creatureRefSchema`/`creatureRefIsEmpty`/`isCastCreatureNpc`/`castCreatureLabel`/`castCreatureWriteRefusal`/`npcCreatureRef`/`libraryCreatureKey`/`creatureIdentityForCitation`/`creatureRefForRulebookSource` (creature.ts:36-241), the module pointer `entityBestiarySlotSchema`/`entityKinds[].bestiary`/`bestiarySlotForEntity` (module.ts:396/:468/:748), `creatureRepo.creatureLookups`/`resolveCreatureCitation`/`resolveDerivedNpcStats`/`creatureRefIdentical`/`castCreatureAsNpc`/`tokenCreature`'s ref arm, `entity-batch.libraryCitationForEntity`, plus `spawn-dialog.tsx`, `change-artifact.ts:240`, `runEngine.ts:1426-1467`/`:1668`/`:3737-3748`, `roomBudget.ts:876`, `canvasChat.ts:1849`, `mob-portrait-participants.ts:139`/`:156`, `BattleSurface.tsx:596`. With BOTH pointers gone, the already-listed `derivedStatOrigin`, `resolveCreatureChunk`, `contentIdentityFor`, `rulebookSourceFor`, the `missingRefReason` family and `getChunksByContentHash` lose their LAST callers. **SURVIVES:** the cast line "NPC: X (stats from Bestiary p.132)" = `derivedStatOrigin(npcName, creatureOrigin)` (encounterResolve.ts:209-211) needs only a stored source string plus the NPC's own name — keep it as `derivedStatOrigin(name, storedSource)` or fold it into `rosterReferenceFor`. `BorrowedStatBlock` survives as a COMPONENT but its props change from `{npcName, citation}` to `{npcName, statBlock, sourceLine}`, deleting its async loading/failed/missing arms; `StatBlockCard` is unchanged. **PINS:** in `tests/features/cast-row-borrowed-stats-scan.test.ts` the RESOLUTION arms are genuinely DELETED (`derivedNpcStats(` count `:82`, `DERIVED_READ` holders `:91-96`, the delegation string `:83`) while the composer/renderer arms are REWRITTEN to the new seam (`:70`/`:74` `derivedStatOrigin(` holders+count, the two mount strings `:99-104`, `BORROWED_RENDERER` holders `:105-110`) — the pin's INTENT (one composer, one renderer, no re-implementation) still holds for the copied line, so REWRITE rather than delete. ~10 further cast-path test files need the same treatment (moduleGen-cast, entity-batch-fixed-cast, entity-batch-cast-description, entity-batch-creature-book, finalize-cast-glue, refill-creature-stats, creature-portrait-agreement, creatureRepo, creature-identity-one-rule, change-artifact, entity-intent). **MEASURED SIDE-NOTE:** the export manifest NEVER covered `creatureRef` — `collectDependencies` has no creatureRef arm — so a cast NPC's library dependency was ALREADY untracked; removing the pointer removes an unmanifested cross-instance dependency. **STILL UNDETERMINED:** whether run `pinnedChunkIds`/`ExportPinnedChunk` survive (they are grounding, not mob stats, so they read as surviving). **IMAGE/PORTRAIT CACHE — AN EXPLICIT SUB-QUESTION, RAISED BY THE OWNER** (verbatim: *"At the moment there is that image cache for bestiary mobs. Under the new rule that cache needs rethinking or be abandoned."*). The cache's whole value rests on IDENTITY BEING STABLE ACROSS INSTANCES: a cited creature keys `chunk:<chunkId>` (`libraryCreatureKey`, creature.ts:117) so ONE bestiary creature shares ONE portrait across encounters, while an authored block keys by CONTENT (`contentCreatureKey`/`contentCreatureIdentity`, creature.ts:151/:227) and is therefore its own creature. Naively copying every mob flips every identity to content-derived, so N copies of the same zombie become N identities: the cache stops caching, existing `chunk:`-keyed `creatureImages`/`mobPortraits` rows ORPHAN (db.ts:1030/:1032), and the `uncited/invented` bucketing (mob-portraits-section.tsx:144-162, :404) starts labelling copies as hand-written. **SETTLED (owner, 2026-09-19): the cache is KEPT — the migrated copy carries a RENAMED OPAQUE ORIGIN TOKEN that is an identity key and NOT a resolver.** Measured mechanism: `mobPortraits: 'id, &creatureKey'` (db.ts:1030, UNIQUE creatureKey, `{id, creatureKey, imageId}`) points at ONE GLOBAL-scope (`campaignId:null`) `images` row — one blob per creature key GLOBALLY — while `creatureImages: '[campaignId+creatureKey]'` (db.ts:1032) holds a CAMPAIGN-scoped clone; neither stores bytes, and tokens are stamped by the same function (battleSeed.ts:68). **WHY THE CHOSEN FORM WINS:** if the surviving token KEEPS the `chunk:<id>` shape, `foldCreatureKey`'s id-key branch (creature.ts:187) already returns it unchanged, so NO existing `mobPortraits`/`creatureImages` row needs remapping — sharing, single generation and heal-on-pack-reinstall all survive, the migration is cheap and REVERSIBLE, and the user sees NO change. **REJECTED — ABANDON (content identity only):** IRREVERSIBLE; it deletes a working campaign-delete-immune cross-campaign cache (`mobPortraitCache.ts` is 347 lines, plus `mob-portrait-cache-queue.ts`, `db.mobPortraits` and the canonical-vs-flavor logic); and it STILL shares byte-identical copies, so it buys INCONSISTENT sharing — shared until the first edit, because the content key covers name AND statBlock, so a rename or a stat edit forks the identity and orphans the old row — while STRANDING art whose pack is uninstalled, since a `mobPortraits` row holds ONLY `chunk:<id>` (v20 dropped the chunkId field, db.ts:820-827): such a row CANNOT be remapped at all, and cannot even name its source because no page is stored. It also contradicts a real requirement — per-instance imagery IS wanted, which is why the authored lane (mob-portrait-participants.ts:104) and the cast NPC (creatureRepo.ts:710) exist. **OPTIONAL FOLLOW-UP under the same identity rule:** shared origin PLUS a per-instance override, whose precedence ALREADY exists (an artifact's own cover beats the presentation row, creatureRepo.ts:429-436) and whose host is the per-artifact cover mechanism (`coverImageId`/`imageIds`, entity-image-queue.ts:37). **THE CHOICE STILL FORCES:** `creature-identity-one-rule`'s rulebook arms and `creature-identity-spelling:111` are REWRITTEN (the one-seam intent survives), `creature-portrait-agreement` needs the key swapped, `creature-key-fold` is untouched only while the token keeps the id-key form, and docs/12:625 plus docs/11 D5/D6 record the opposite and are amended in the same landing. **NOT VERIFIED:** the probes ran NO tests (the suite lock was held), so every pin claim above is a file/assertion READING and not a red/green result; and the live IndexedDB could not be read, so how many `chunk:`-keyed rows exist remains UNKNOWN. **CENTRALIZATION IS A BINDING ACCEPTANCE CRITERION, NOT A SIDE EFFECT** (owner-directed, verbatim: *"I do not want this to lead to code fragmentation, quite the opposite. Code should get more centralized because of these changes since there are less special cases. So, i expect a MORE centralized outcome out of this, an easier to maintain model."*). Every brief in this arc carries the clause below, and the landing report must carry the MEASURED before/after:
1. **ONE representation, and NO compatibility path.** The variant discriminants must go to ZERO outside migration code. Measured at this HEAD in `src/`: `'rulebook'` 40 hits in 22 files, `'npc-ref'` 35 in 21, `creatureRef` 82 in 19, and 16 files branch on the source union; the resolver family `resolveCreatureCitation` 14 in 5, `resolveCreatureChunk` 4 in 2, `isMissingRefOrigin` 7 in 3, `missingRefReason` 6 in 1. A "legacy pointer still read for old rows" is FRAGMENTATION, not a migration: the migration REWRITES the rows, it does not add a second reader.
2. **ONE seam per surviving idea, each with an EXACTLY-ONE pin** (centralization obligation 2). The survivors and their counts today: identity `rosterEntryCreatureIdentity` 9 hits in 3 files; stat-block source `rosterStatBlockFor`; source-line composition `rosterReferenceFor` 12 in 4 (already guarded by `roster-reference-parity.test.ts`'s source scan, which must KEEP passing); portrait lookup via the cover seam. Every call site goes THROUGH them; no caller composes its own.
3. **The duplication baseline must SHRINK and must never grow.** Measured at this HEAD: `duplicateImplementationsBaseline.json` (src) **14 groups / 32 sites**; `duplicateImplementationsTestsBaseline.json` (tests) **136 / 390**; cross-tree **0**. This arc ALREADY owns one OWED entry — `src/db/creatureRepo.ts:getChunkByContentHash` + `src/db/monsterResolve.ts:getChunkByContentHash`, which the baseline itself marks *"fold onto one db helper. Owed; out of this detector slice's scope"* — and BOTH copies are in this arc's DIES list, so landing must DELETE that baseline line rather than bless it. A new copy reds LANDED; a folded copy reds as a stale entry until its line is removed.
4. **"More centralized" must be a MEASUREMENT, not a claim.** The landing reports the before/after for the counts in (1), (2) and (3). A landing that leaves them unchanged has not done this arc, whatever else it fixed.
 **MIGRATION RECIPE — MEASURED FROM THE v20 PRECEDENT, replacing the discovery's INFERRED half** (read-only probe, docs/17 row 248). The mechanism to copy: `this.version(20).stores({…}).upgrade(async (tx) => { … await repairCreatureCitations({ tx }); })` (db.ts:796-834 → creatureRepair.ts:77-280). The seam takes the Dexie TRANSACTION and NEVER the `db` singleton, because "the `version(20)` upgrade body runs before the upgraded `db` instance is usable, and a nested `db.transaction` there fails" (creatureRepair.ts:36-40). Rows it cannot convert are NAMED, not dropped (`report.unconverted.push({where,name,reason})`, :157-161, :190-194); it throws ONLY when the report has nowhere to land — *"this workspace has retired creature rows but no settings row to report the outcome in — refusing to migrate silently"* (:271-275); the report lands in `settings` (:278) and reaches the user once via AppShell.tsx:121-139. Secondary-index queries inside an upgrade body are precedented (db.ts:514, :551). Idempotency = one transaction plus a marker that no longer exists. **STEPS:** (1) a new seam `src/db/mobCopyRepair.ts` `repairMobCopies({tx})` plus its db.ts `.upgrade(...)`, over artifacts/chunks/rulebooks/modules/campaigns/settings; (2) ROSTER ARM — for `source.type === 'rulebook'` (artifact.ts:301-335) call the PURE `resolveCreatureChunk(citation, lookups)` (encounterResolve.ts:164) with TX-BACKED lookups, compose the label with `await creatureOriginLabel(chunk, creatureCitationName(source, entry.name), lookups)` (:187-201 — needs only `getRulebook`, domain-pure with injected lookups, :4-9), then write `{type:'inline', statBlock: chunk.statBlock}`, the attribution, and the token `chunk:<chunk.id>` (libraryCreatureKey, creature.ts:116), dropping `source`; `statBlock == null` is a failure arm; (3) NPC ARM — `data.creatureRef` (artifact.ts:231) resolves identically, and its visible line is `derivedStatOrigin(npcName, creatureOrigin)` (encounterResolve.ts:209-211); write statBlock + attribution + token (the refine at :241 forbids both); (4) MODULE ARM — `entityKinds[].bestiary` `{creature, book?}` (module.ts:396-409, :468) needs the campaign's `system` before the same chunk/label read; (5) TOKEN — keep the `chunk:` shape, since `foldCreatureKey` returns ids unchanged (creature.ts:187), so NO `mobPortraits`/`creatureImages` remap is needed; (6) TESTS mirror `tests/db/migration.test.ts`: seed a legacy Dexie with `.stores()`, `await import('@/db/db')` + `db.open()` (:1327-1347, :1468-1516), assert `db.verno`, the row writes, the EXACT settings report, a second run reporting all-zero via an ordinary `db.transaction('rw',[…])` calling the seam (:1518-1543), and a non-vacuity zero case (v21 :1621-1633). **FAILURE ARM — OWNER-DECIDED 2026-09-19: CONVERT WHAT RESOLVES, AND REPORT THE REST AS UNRESOLVED.** A mob that cannot be converted (pack uninstalled, chunk gone, `statBlock == null`) becomes a LOUD, NAMED unresolved entry surfaced through the existing missing-ref idiom/banner — never a silent or placeholder stat block — plus a STARTUP RETRY so that reinstalling the pack heals it later, which the repo already has a precedent for (the startup reconcile). "Refuse to open, naming the pack" was REJECTED as SELF-DEFEATING: pack import happens INSIDE the app, so a blocked upgrade would leave the user unable to install the pack that unblocks it. Keeping the legacy arms alive instead was rejected as the permanent fragmentation the owner forbade. **LOUD UNKNOWNS THE WRITER MUST RESOLVE, NOT ASSUME:** (a) only `repairCreatureCitations` is PROVEN awaitable inside an upgrade transaction — the pure domain functions are tx-backable by INSPECTION, not by proof; (b) the `db`-BOUND wrappers are NOT callable in-transaction — `creatureRepo.resolveCreatureCitation` (:126), `resolveMonsterEntryWithRepos` (monsterResolve.ts:17), `creatureLookups()` (:79) — and `libraryCitationForEntity` (entity-batch.ts:239) reaches `listLibraryCreatures` → `db.rulebooks` (creatureRepo.ts:287-296), so the MODULE arm needs an in-transaction path built for it; (c) await-safety in-tx is a WRITER-VERIFIED question with a test, not an assumption; (d) the count of pointer-mobs in the owner's live DB is UNKNOWN. **WRITER FINDINGS AT DISPATCH (2026-09-19, writer 8f1bd0d0) — corrections to this recipe, recorded here rather than left in the thread.** (1) **VERSION DRIFT: the recipe said v21; the real highest Dexie version at `origin/main` is 23** (`this.version(23)`, `db.ts:1016`, ideaBoards; v22 was the creature-key fold), so the migration is **version(24)** and the recipe's test line anchors must be re-derived. The mechanism itself was CONFIRMED correct (tx-taking seam, settings report, AppShell toast). (2) **THE POINTER SURVIVES AS THE RETRY FORM, and the recipe did not say so.** A plain deletion of the pointer arms contradicts this recipe's OWN failure arm: a startup retry must know WHICH chunk to retry, so on an UNCONVERTIBLE row the `rulebook` pointer stays, and it must stay readable by the missing-ref banner; a CONVERTED row carries NO pointer. DISPATCHER RESOLUTION: the pointer survives narrowly as the retry handle, pinned by tests (converted row has none; unconvertible row keeps one AND is named in the settings report). The alternative — delete it and lose the retry — is REJECTED. (3) **SLICE SPLIT, because the full arc is measurably not one turn**: `'rulebook'` 40 hits across 22 files, `'npc-ref'` 35 across 21, `creatureRef` 75 refs, 20 `src` files in the union, ~10 test files to rewrite. **SLICE 1 (in flight)** = migration + stamping: roster + NPC arms, the stamped source line, the `chunk:` origin token, the settings report, the startup retry, focused tests. **REMAINING SLICE** = the MODULE arm (`entityKinds[].bestiary`, a NAME pointer) plus the shape swap/deletion across consumers. LOUD UNKNOWN (b) is CONFIRMED, not assumed: in-tx resolution of that NAME pointer would need a SECOND copy of the library name-matching, so the remaining slice MUST extend the ONE existing name-matching seam rather than copy it — a second copy is exactly the fragmentation the owner forbids.
 **SUGGESTED SLICING (dispatcher's):** migration + stamping first (it creates the copies and the attribution), then the shape swap and the deletion, then the attribution rewrite in the renderers.

RULE: inside a hardlink clone `rm` the file FIRST, then write a fresh one; VERIFY with `ls -li` that the worktree copy and the main tree copy have DIFFERENT inodes; same for `node_modules/.tmp/*.tsbuildinfo`. AND: when the gate says `TYPECHECK FAILED` with no type error naming the changed files, READ `<gate-logdir>/typecheck.log` BEFORE touching anything.

Grounding: the NPC-only `appearance` shortcut is VERBATIM and uncapped; otherwise `A <system> illustration of <name> (<kind>).` + Summary + a `Description:` capped at 800 chars; nothing to ground on throws loudly. **THE CROP IS CONFIRMED AND IS OURS**: `imagePromptDraft.ts:189` `description.slice(0, 800)` and `:138` `parts.join('\n').slice(0, 800)` are HARD, SILENT cuts — no ellipsis, no note, no counter — and the truncated string IS `draft.prompt`, so it is STORED on `images.prompt` and SENT; a retry/resume reuses the cropped draft and seeds the edit textarea from it. THE PRECEDENT TO COPY IS THE MOB ARC: an authored copy, the composed origin line stamped as data, and the `chunk:` token kept as an OPAQUE ORIGIN TOKEN so nothing has to remap. **DISCOVERY DONE (read-only probe `d8fdbd1c`, base `14e63fb`, report `.gate-logs/discoveries/module-core-references.md`). THREE FINDINGS, the second of which changes the arc's SHAPE.** (1) **THE SEAM ANSWER:** ONE copy-on-write seam CAN carry the library-CONTENT families — (A) a roster `rulebook` citation, (A2) an NPC `creatureRef`, (B) the module `entityKinds[].bestiary` slot (its fulfilment IS A/A2), (C) stat-block spell names. They are ONE operation: a payload off a `chunks` row + the read-time-composed origin line STAMPED + the opaque `chunk:<id>` token — proposed home `domain/libraryCopy.ts` (pure and tx-callable, the `domain/libraryCreature` shape) with `db/libraryCopyRepair.ts({tx,reason})`, a `version(25).upgrade` and the start-up retry, i.e. the row-248 precedent. **FOUR families genuinely DIFFER and must NOT be forced in:** (E) global library artifact refs (`npc-ref` / `links[].targetId` — artifact ADOPTION, not a payload copy; 106 hits / 32 files), (F) images (already carried by export AND backup), (G) run `personaId` (the provenance of an ACT, not a citation; 48 hits / 9 files), (H) run `pinnedChunkIds` (advisory BY MANIFEST DESIGN; 37 hits / 10 files). (2) **THE ONE-SHOT MIGRATION CANNOT SATISFY THE RULE — THE APP KEEPS MINTING POINTERS.** `db/creatureRepo.ts:688 castCreatureAsNpc` (the module generator's ONLY cast path, via `features/modules/entity-batch.ts:671`) writes `creatureRef` on every NEW npc; `runEngine.ts:1552-1575` mints a fresh roster `rulebook` citation for EVERY generated encounter; `monster-source.tsx:216` and `spawn-picker-logic.ts:113` mint more; `runEngine.ts:1495` preserves `creatureRef` through a refill. And the retry cannot heal any of them, because `AppShell.tsx:182-190` runs `retryMobCopies()` ONLY when `settings.mobCopyRepair.unconverted` is non-empty — which on a healthy workspace it never is. So: **COPY-ON-WRITE AT WRITE TIME IS THE REAL FIX, AND THE UPGRADE IS ITS BACKFILL HALF.** That is the arc's NEXT slice, and it is SERIALIZED behind row 248c because it touches the same files 248c is deleting across (measured overlap: 56 test files / 41 src files in the union). (3) **`originToken` IS NOT INERT** — `domain/creature.rosterEntryCreatureIdentity` (`creature.ts:295-307`) feeds `token.creatureKey` at seed (`battleSeed.ts:274`) and `BattleSurface.tsx:539-555` / `:2181` resolves the token card through `tokenCreature` → a LIVE chunk read (`creatureRepo.ts:444-489`), so a MIGRATED mob's battle card still disappears with the pack; the docs/18 §5 claim is corrected and 248c is STEERED with it. ALSO RECORDED, adjacent but a DIFFERENT class: the dependency manifest is BLIND to `creatureRef` (0 occurrences in `exportDependencies.ts` / `exportImport.ts`), so an unmigrated cast NPC imports with NO warning and then shows `missing ref`; version-drift (same title, different hash) counts as NOT present, so another rulebook version blocks an import exactly like a missing book (`exportDependencies.ts:284-286`, `:386`); and import RE-IDS artifacts but never remaps `module.documentPlan`'s artifactId/companion refs (`exportImport.ts:726-739` vs `:785-788`), so every module with a plan falls back loudly to the procedural outline after a load — the same "save/load dependency" class the owner named, and its own slice. DECIDED-OWNER | id=dice-engine-release-between-uses | decision=**KEEP THE RELEASE** (dispatcher default under the owner blanket "go with your recommendation", VETOABLE IN ONE COMMIT). KEEP, because on a fanless tablet with WebKit strict GPU and canvas budgets, residency is the resource that decides whether a long session survives — and a bounded re-init is the cheaper failure. AWAITING-OWNER | id=unconvertible-row-isolation-fork | what=**THE ONLY STORED REFERENCES THAT STILL BLOCK AN IMPORT ARE THE UNCONVERTIBLE-ROW POINTERS — and they exist because the owner chose them.** The legacy roster `rulebook`/`npc-ref` arms are kept on rows whose pack was missing at migration time so the start-up retry can heal them later (his own failure-arm decision). But they are exactly the two arms the IMPORT MANIFEST checks: an `npc-ref` to a GLOBAL row is ALWAYS BLOCKING with no content-hash escape, so ONE unadopted row refuses the whole campaign import, and a `rulebook` citation blocks when missing. OPTIONS: **(i) KEEP the pointer and make it NON-BLOCKING and LOUD** (the drift-policy precedent the owner already accepted) — cheap, loses nothing, never refuses an import, but the pointer stays stored, so isolation is complete-except-unconvertible-rows; **(ii) CONVERT with the stats lost** and the named `sourceLine` kept — complete isolation, but a mob whose pack was missing ends up with no stat block, which is a real loss and against the no-placeholder rule; **(iii) QUARANTINE the unconvertible rows into an inert table** — the main tables become completely reference-free, the pointer survives where the retry can still read it, and nothing is lost; more work, and the only option that satisfies BOTH complete isolation and the retry. **DISPATCHER RECOMMENDATION: (iii)**, because the owner has twice insisted on COMPLETE and it is the only arm that does not trade his retry away; (i) is the cheap fallback and should be named as NOT complete. | date=2026-09-20 SUPERSEDED-IN-INTENT 2026-09-20 by DECIDED-OWNER `clean-cut-no-migrations`: with the migration layer abolished there ARE no unconvertible rows, so all three arms die with it. This fork stays OPEN only until that landing lands, and must NOT be put to the owner again.

## Guards (why this workflow has teeth)

```
GUARD | failure=stale workers | where=the DSH harness: the core provides `send_message`/`interrupt_agent`/`list_agents` — the WHOLE surface of `@deepseek-ai/dsh-tool-subagent-control`, which offers no delete of any kind — AND the community plugin `dsh-plugin-subagent-delete` is now INSTALLED on this box (`github:heiheiha798/dsh-plugin-subagent-delete#v0.2.0`, added 2026-09-19 with `dsh plugin --profile web add`, docs/17 row 245), adding `delete_subagent` (permanent: stop, detach, remove the on-disk session log and the projection row), `release_subagent` (stop, keep the transcript) and `list_subagents` (descendants INCLUDING finished one-shot ones, which the core `list_agents` omits — exactly the population that accumulates). The on-disk registry is still `~/.dsh/sessions/<this repo's slug>`. | verify=(1) `dsh --profile web --dump-config | grep subagent-delete` shows the composed row; (2) IN A SESSION STARTED AFTER A PROFILE RESTART — the plugin's tools DO NOT EXIST in a session older than the install, which is a fact about that session and not a defect — call `list_subagents`, then `delete_subagent` on a throwaway probe and expect it gone from `list_subagents` AND its session directory removed. **RUN AND PASSED 2026-09-19 (docs/17 row 246):** probe `7e10880f-…` was deleted with `dirRemoved`/`projRemoved`/`removed_from_ui_list` all true, then re-checked INDEPENDENTLY — `list_subagents` empty, the directory count back to its baseline, BOTH uuid spellings gone (on this box the directory is the BARE uuid, `~/.dsh/sessions/<slug>/<uuid>`, not `session-<uuid>`), and no trace of the id in the session store. **DO NOT CHECK THAT LAST ARM WITH A PLAIN `grep -rl`:** the session logs are zstd-COMPRESSED, so a plain grep finds nothing EVEN BEFORE the delete and the check is VACUOUS — a second independent read-only agent caught exactly that error in this row's first draft. Decompress (`zstd -dc` over every `session*.zstd`) and expect the only residual hits to be the DELETING session's own transcript, which legitimately records the spawn and the delete; no projection row for the probe may remain. RE-RUN THIS AFTER EVERY DSH OR PLUGIN UPDATE: both the harness and this plugin are code under the profile layer, and the plugin is third-party; (3) `bash scripts/board.sh` for the writer-liveness half, which resolves every IN-FLIGHT writer against `~/.dsh/sessions/<slug>` and flags a session log written in the last 6h that the board does not name
GUARD | failure=RAM / worker storms | where=scripts/gate.sh (atomic lock; at most TWO chunks at once, one worker each in its own `setsid` group; `tests/features` split so the long tails overlap; COMBINED-RSS watchdog at 3000MB with a 90% soft FALLBACK to sequential before the kill line; a VOID chunk re-run sequentially and never counted; a per-run union check that the chunk lists are exactly the test files, none twice; diff-scoped ORDER + exactly two skips — docs-only runs no vitest, tests-only runs only its chunk) + AGENTS §Host hygiene 1–7 | verify=`bash scripts/board.sh` (lock owner + orphans + load), `GATE_PLAN_ONLY=1 bash scripts/gate.sh` (prints the plan, runs nothing), and never hand-roll a gate
GUARD | failure=context / compaction | where=DSH, through the profile patch layer: the model route is `deepseek-flash`, and compaction is configured by `~/.dsh/profiles/web/cordis.patch.yml` — the predecessor's pins were `compaction-basic` (maxTokens 65536, compactionRetries 2, summarizer z-ai/glm-5.3-flash) because the 8192 default truncated large-span summaries and failed with "Compaction could not produce a useful summary". MEASURED 2026-09-19: on THIS box that file exists but is an EMPTY patch, so NO compaction pins are applied here — the predecessor's numbers are history, not this box's configuration. | verify=`cat ~/.dsh/profiles/web/cordis.patch.yml` — an empty patch means no pins, and the guard then rests on the CoS keeping its thread small on purpose (AGENTS §Chief of staff, context budget), because a session too large to compact cannot be recovered
GUARD | id=the-inflight-row-dies-in-the-landing-commit | how=THE IN-FLIGHT ROW AND THE LANDED ROW ARE ONE RECORD AT TWO TIMES, so the commit that records a landing MUST delete its IN-FLIGHT row in the SAME commit. This has bitten TWICE in one session — row 247's row still claimed a RUNNING writer with ~20 modified files after its landing and retirement, and row 253's survived its own verification and retirement — and a stale IN-FLIGHT row is not harmless: the board is machine-parsed and read by `scripts/board.sh`, so it reports a live writer that does not exist, and a successor reconciling from the board has to disprove it before dispatching. The check is cheap and mechanical: after recording any landing, `grep -c '^IN-FLIGHT' docs/20-ORCHESTRATION.md` must equal the number of writers actually in flight (ZERO or ONE at dispatch time, never a leftover). **ADDENDUM (2026-09-19), learned by a dispatcher script failing three times:** because the LANDING commit deletes the IN-FLIGHT row, the dispatcher's NEXT dispatch must INSERT its row rather than REPLACING the previous one — a "replace the stale row" script has no stale row to find and throws (which is how this was learned: the 255a landing had already deleted its own row, exactly as this guard demands).
GUARD | id=no-baseline-edits-means-no-gains | how=A BRIEF THAT SAYS "NO `*Baseline.json` EDITS" IS WRONG AS WRITTEN, and row 263 proved it: the mandated fold removed a baselined duplicate, which reddens the tripwire as a STALE entry whose own recorded reason said to fold it — and the tripwire demands the line be DELETED. A writer obeying the blanket ban would have had to leave the tree red. THE CORRECT INSTRUCTION IS **NO BASELINE GAINS**: the direction permitted is DELETE-ONLY (a folded copy deletes its entry; nothing may ever be ADDED, because an addition blesses a new duplicate). Verify it the same way this row was verified: `git diff --numstat <landing>^ <landing> -- <baseline>` must show ZERO insertions, and the removed entry must be one whose recorded reason named the fold that just happened.
GUARD | id=a-landing-closes-its-reservation | how=A LANDED SLICE MUST CLOSE ITS OWN `QUEUE` RESERVATION in the same board commit, or the queue lies about what is left. Measured 2026-09-20 at the row-272 reconcile: TEN `QUEUE` lines still carried `row RESERVED **n**` for rows that had LANDED (248 is a live partial and stays open; 255 stays open as the umbrella; **257, 260 and 271 are now `QUEUE-CLOSED`**), and two more described probes as IN FLIGHT months-of-work after their reports landed (`255`, `262` — both corrected on this pass). A successor reading the queue could not tell live work from history. VERIFY: every `QUEUE`/`QUEUE-CLOSED` line either names a row with no `LANDED` record, or states in its own text why it is still open (a partial landing, an umbrella, a decision); `grep -o '^QUEUE | .*row RESERVED \*\*[0-9]*\*\*' docs/20-ORCHESTRATION.md` (LIVE reservations only — a `QUEUE-CLOSED` line deliberately keeps its original text, so scanning every line would flag the closed ones) must not name a row that already has a LANDED record.
GUARD | id=in-repo-scratch-must-be-lint-ignored | how=EVERY in-repo directory that can hold scratch SOURCE files must be in `eslint.config.js` ignores, because the main tree lint run walks the whole repo. `worktrees` was there; `.gate-logs` was NOT, and nothing noticed until the dispatcher preserved a retired writer evidence there — which includes `.ts`/`.tsx` injection backups — and the compile tier failed with TWO PARSING ERRORS that had nothing to do with the change under test. Fixed at the source (`ignores` gained `.gate-logs`) rather than by renaming evidence, because the next evidence copy would have hit the same wall. A scratch directory that is only safe while nobody puts source files in it is not safe.
GUARD | id=green-reaches-the-badge | how=THE DISPATCHER'S GREEN MUST REACH THE RECORD THE BADGE READS, or the app lies about its own verification. `scripts/buildStatus.mjs` (docs/17 row 250) resolves the deployed badge from `LANDED | … | verify=… GATE GREEN` records only (newest on HEAD's ancestry by commit distance), and since the row-252 split a WRITER writes its LANDED row with its COMPILE tier — so the DISPATCHER's post-gate board commit must add a GATE GREEN record **whose `sha` is the GATED TREE TIP** (see the row-272 LANDED record). MEASURED DEFECT this fixes, caught 2026-09-20 while reconciling: the newest GATE GREEN record was row 250 (`3ebdb42`), so the LIVE payload at `futuremagic.de/Campaigner/build-status.json` read `wip — 180 changed file(s) since the last GATE GREEN landing 3ebdb42 are not documentation-only` while the full suite was green over that same stretch (reproduced locally with `node scripts/buildStatus.mjs --board <(git show origin/main:docs/20-ORCHESTRATION.md) --head origin/main`). VERIFY: that command must print `verified` after a deploy of a gated tree, or `wip` with a TRUE reason; a green gate recorded only in the reconciled header is the defect this GUARD exists for.
GUARD | id=job-wake-budget | how=A SETTLED BACKGROUND JOB STOPS WAKING THE SESSION AFTER THREE CONSECUTIVE COMPLETIONS with no user message in between — the `tool-jobs` default `maxConsecutiveWakes: 3`, reset only by a real user message (docs/17 row 273). The symptom reads as "the agent is not being woken by long jobs", which is why it is a GUARD and not a footnote: an autonomous stretch of gates and probes goes silent while jobs keep finishing. FIXED 2026-09-20 by the user preset `longrun` (`~/.dsh/.agent-presets/longrun/`, a copy of `standard` with `maxConsecutiveWakes: 100`) selected as the default in `~/.dsh/settings.yaml`; the Web profile DISABLES the host-plane `tool-jobs` row, so the profile patch layer cannot reach it — a preset is the only place the live row can be configured. VERIFY: `python3 -c "import yaml;"` is not the check — the check is that the file was parsed with the `!!js` tags tolerated and that `diff` against the shipped preset names ONLY the config block; then a NEW session must be seen to wake past three completions. A session that already mounted its preset keeps the old budget until it is restarted, so the guard applies to the session you are in.
GUARD | id=quote-the-failure-text-first | how=THE DISPATCHER THEORISED THREE WRONG MECHANISMS IN ONE SESSION (2026-09-20): "fresh origin" (that the purge never ran), "the first-run wizard covered the notice", and "shared `tsbuildinfo` corrupted the typecheck". All three were PLAUSIBLE, all three were WRONG, and in every case the deciding evidence sat in a file not yet read — most sharply the third, where the gate own `typecheck.log` said `pnpm install` / `[ERR_SQLITE_ERROR]` while the dispatcher had already ordered two repairs and re-run the gate twice on the theory. A symptom names WHERE to look, never WHAT happened. | rule=**A failure diagnosis begins by QUOTING THE FAILURE TEXT** — the gate raw log, the raw error, the real DOM — and never by naming a mechanism. Reading it inside a BACKGROUND job does not count: the text must be in hand BEFORE the next command is issued. Two of the three wrong mechanisms were written into the board as fact before being measured; both are retracted in place rather than deleted, so the record shows the error and its correction.
GUARD | id=the-injection-sanity-gate-covers-tests | how=A LONE `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` DOES NOT TYPECHECK `tests/**`, so an injection arm whose sanity gate is written that way can report "clean" over a test tree that does not compile — and the arm's RED/GREEN then means nothing. MEASURED 2026-09-20 by the row-287 writer, whose FIRST compile tier was exit 1 on TWO real test-file type errors (an unused `persona` destructure and a `VALID_STATBLOCK` literal whose `system` widened to `string`) that no app-project `--noEmit` run would have named. The injection sanity gate is therefore `tsc -b` (which builds every project, the app AND the tests) or the gate's own compile tier — and the dispatcher's own arms were re-run for exactly this reason: two of them were tsc-DIRTY on their first attempt and were recorded VOID rather than counted.
```
